Fate of the Galaxy is a huge, fully-featured Star Wars space combat sim that, thanks to being a total conversion mod for Freespace Open, even runs on my rubbish laptop

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Star Wars has been back in the news recently because, to cut a long story short, the US alt-right have decided that Darth Vader was actually the good guy. We are not here to indulge such buffoonery. We are here to talk about Fate of the Galaxy, a free unofficial space combat simulator set during the events of the original Star Wars trilogy, featuring several mission-based campaigns and a stable of Imperial and Rebel Alliance ships that ranges from the quad-blasting X-Wing to Boba Fett's Firespray 31. Also, Naboo starfighters, if you really must.

It's a total conversion of poor dead Volition's venerable dogfighter FreeSpace 2, running on the open source Free Space 2 Open engine via the popular Knossos launcher. You don't need a copy of FreeSpace 2 to play it. You also don't need to pay. And nor do you need a whizbang PC – this bucket of bolts hums along happily on my i5-1145G7 with no dedicated graphics card. As the Rancor said to the padawan: finally, some good food.

Fate of the Galaxy has been in development off and on for almost two decades, weathering a number of team changes and shifts of focus. The FreeSpace Wiki has a longer account of its travels. It's not an officially licensed Star Wars production, as made evident by a scrolling text intro which takes the official orchestral theme and moves every note up or down a place, as though spitefully jolting the piano around under John Williams' fingers. It's not quite as bizarre as Portsmouth Sinfonia's Also sprach Zarathustra but it did make me cackle like the Emperor contemplating a freshly made asteroid field. But then I began playing, and my presumptuous merriment became a frown of concentration.

While hardly Steel Battalion, Fate of the Galaxy is a proper nerdy sim with a bustling 3D cockpit and all sorts of keyboard shortcuts to try once and immediately forget. You'll need to funnel energy between fore and aft shields during bombing runs on Star Destroyers, target individual subsystems with your ion cannon, give orders to other pilots, and consider whether to synch different guns for maximum effect. I can run the game fine on my work laptop with integrated graphics, but I'm definitely feeling the need for a beefier keyboard. And also a joystick. Certainly, I'm not going to get far in LAN multiplayer with this hunk of junk.

It seems a very generous production, based on 30 minutes playing kiss-chase with TIE bombers. The story campaigns and quickfix stand-alone skirmishes aside, there's a mission editor with text-based files for easier sharing. There's also VR support with head-tracking, should you own one of those haunted helmets. Learn more via the online manual (Google Doc), and find Fate of the Galaxy itself on Moddb. You'll need around seven gigs of disc space.

This being the year 2026, there's the question of generative AI usage - a topic of much concern lately in modding circles. Fate of the Galaxy's official site boasts that all the visual assets were "made by humans only". I'm less sure about other aspects – there's seemingly some artificial VO during the tutorial, though it sounds like antiquated, less insidious robocaller software. The FreeSpace Open engine itself greatly pre-dates today's chatbot invasion. The original FreeSpace 2 launched in 1999 - a more civilised age, when everybody still thought Darth Vader was an arsehole.

Researchers asked people to use AI chatbots for election info – results were mixed

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Chatbots can skew election-related information and act as an intervening layer in a voter's search.

Most search tools now put a layer of AI between you and the information you’re looking for, especially Google.

Also: Tired of AI Overviews? I found 9 Google Search alternatives

In a new study, Yale researchers found that a third of users asked a chatbot to help them pick a candidate. Grok often recommended candidates on its own without any information from users, while ChatGPT recommended candidates most often overall, and twice as much as Claude.

The researchers asked nearly 2,500 people in 35 states with Senate races to query ChatGPT, Claude, Grok, or Gemini about the election. Participants in the study, which ran for a few weeks in October leading up to the Nov. 3 midterm elections, were adults who already use chatbots weekly.

Most users asked chatbots for basic facts about the election, including who was on the ballot. But 36% asked a chatbot to help them pick a candidate to vote for, often by describing their priorities and asking the chatbot to assess which candidates aligned with them.

The former query has slightly less room for error, because the internet has a ton of data on the basic facts of an election, such as who is running where. Chatbots interpreting candidate positions, on the other hand, is a bit more dicey, and relies significantly on the sources a chatbot uses (a statement from a candidate’s own website, for example, is more reliable than a social media clip of an opponent characterizing them in a certain way).

Also: How to keep your chatbot conversations as private as possible

In one example, a user asked ChatGPT to give them the “pros and cons” of each candidate in Virginia, an inherently subjective question.

When it came to recommending candidates, chatbots varied widely.

“When users asked who to vote for but had not yet said anything about themselves, only Grok regularly recommended a candidate, in 14% of chats,” the researchers wrote. “Once users shared their views, ChatGPT recommended a candidate in 79% of chats and Claude in 40%, with Grok and Gemini in between.”

Pew Research found that 42% of US adults surveyed use chatbots to search for information. That’s a broad umbrella that can include election information — but Pew also found that only 8% of US adults reported using chatbots to find election-related information in this year’s election.

What those users are looking for matters, though. Pew found that of that group, 76% were inquiring about where candidates stand on certain issues, and 71% were fact-checking information they’d heard about candidates or issues.

Also: OpenAI will merge ChatGPT’s chat and work tabs

Given that chatbots are still not reliable summarizers, the nature of these questions poses a misinformation risk if users don’t also check a chatbot’s linked sources. An overwhelming majority of users don’t, at least when it comes to Google’s AI Overviews. At least one study found AI platforms gave users “outdated or inaccurate” information that could mislead voters in some cases by missing a deadline or failing to mention a step about voting logistics.

This election cycle was particularly plagued by AI-generated deepfakes and misinformation as tools have become much more sophisticated since the 2024 election. OpenAI and Anthropic reportedly set up election “war rooms” to monitor misuse and information accuracy, according to exclusive reporting by Politico and Semafor.

While an August study from the Brennan Center for Justice found that chatbots did push back surprisingly well against certain falsehoods related to election disinformation campaigns, it also found that AI models easily generated “misleading election-related images and video” when asked.

“Many of their safeguards for preventing this were ineffective, inconsistently applied, or functionally absent,” noted the study, which tested ChatGPT, Gemini, Grok, Meta AI, Runway, and Flux.2.

Also: 50 AI agents tried to build a thriving Minecraft civilization – it didn’t end well

Apart from these factors, however, there is a broader theme in AI use that is equally consequential for civic engagement: cognitive offloading. The political media landscape can already be dense and hard to parse for a voter trying to stay informed. Layering a chatbot — which has opaque training data sets, filters, and other settings determined at the discretion of AI labs — on top of that information search process can further muddy the waters while appearing like a convenient and neutral summarizing tool.

While some studies test how AI chatbots respond to election questions by using standardized questions, this approach let users themselves engage with chatbots as they normally would. While the Yale researchers’ queries and standardized study queries show a lot of similarities, the distinction is valuable for evaluations.

“This is a small step toward evaluations that measure AI’s political influence on the questions users actually ask,” the researchers noted.

Outside of users self-reporting what they asked a chatbot, only AI labs have records of what users are really asking about elections. In October, Anthropic analyzed 250,000 conversations with Claude and determined people “rarely ask Claude whom or what to vote for,” finding that “0.51% of all conversations are specifically about elections or voting,” though it expected election-related queries to increase closer to Nov. 3.

Also: 6 AI agent workflows I use to hand off everyday tasks

“More often, we observed people asking Claude questions that could help them reach their own conclusions, like where candidates stand on a particular issue. Requests that violated our election integrity policies were even less common, appearing in about 0.002% of conversations,” Anthropic’s report said.

The Yale researchers said the next step for a study like theirs is determining how accurate and unbiased models are in their responses, as well as what models learn about users, and whether their recommendations actually match users’ needs.

“In multi-turn chats, users often answered the chatbot’s questions, and those answers led to its recommendations. The questions a model asks shape what users tell it and what it recommends,” the researchers explained.

3 Expert-Approved Exercises to Build Power in Your Climbing

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

There’s a famous quote by Wolfgang Güllich, the German climber who invented the word “redpoint”: “There’s no such thing as too much power.” As a coach, I often see and hear this in the climbing community. I agree with the sentiment, but I think that by and large, power has been misused, misunderstood, and poorly trained by the vast majority of climbers.

Firstly, I think it’s misused and misunderstood because the general climbing population doesn’t grasp that strength and power are not the same thing. Secondly, as a result of this, the training methods climbers use when they’re trying to improve their power are often wrong.

So let’s start with a very simple explanation of strength and power. I’m also going to have to bring speed into the equation, as this gives context for how these all sit on a spectrum. Afterwards, we’ll look at some of the main things I think climbers are getting wrong when doing their own power training, how to solve these issues, and some examples of simple but effective ways to train power.

The reason that power matters so much in climbing is because not all moves are possible—or most efficient—when executed at a slower pace without speed and power. Trying to just use strength (slower movement) could mean that you’ll either fail on the move or you’ll climb inefficiently using excess energy, which ultimately means no send!

An easy example of this is completing a super long hard move. With just strength, we’re only able to lock to a certain depth of shoulder position and throw or deadpoint to a limited distance. Add in power and you’ll cover way more distance and end up latching that hold.

One additional note on the “power endurance” term: This one is a bit of a misnomer which caused us many issues over the years at Lattice, mainly because power endurance does not actually use power. It should have been named “strength endurance,” as that’s what it really is.

Strength is how much force you can produce. Think of a max hang on a small edge, or a heavy weighted pull-up. The movement is slow, but the force is high.

Speed is how fast you can move when there’s very little resistance. Think of how quickly you can move your hand from one hold to the next with only the weight of your own arm to overcome.

Power sits between the two. It’s force multiplied by speed, so you only have lots of it when you can produce a relatively high force quickly. Dynos, band-assisted pull-ups, and latching holds at the end of a deadpoint all fit into this category.

Think of it as a sliding scale. Heavy and slow climbing is at one end, light and fast climbing is at the other, and power is in the middle. You can’t produce your maximum force and your maximum speed at the same time, so where you train on that scale matters and I think climbers are continually getting this wrong.

None of this means strength doesn’t matter. Strength is the foundation of power. A stronger and bigger muscle can produce more force at every speed, so if you’re relatively weak, getting stronger will improve your power more than anything else. This is a really important point to grasp, for those of you who haven’t yet developed a foundation of high strength. Once you’re reasonably strong, though, specific power training starts to give you more benefits. As a rough guide, if you can’t do a pull-up with an extra 35-50% of your bodyweight, strength should be the main focus still (even though power training in addition is still useful). Once you’re above this threshold, power training should become increasingly important.

This is the big one. Power is extremely sensitive to fatigue and it drops well before you actually feel tired. Despite this, many climbers I see at the gym do their “power work” at the end of a session, after an hour of board climbing or a set of endurance drills. Unfortunately, if every rep is slower than your best, you’re practicing being slow. Power work goes at the start of the session, straight after a thorough warm-up, when you’re fresh. Prioritise this timing over everything.

Linked laps on the campus board, long sets of explosive pull-ups, and circuits of “powerful” moves can be useful, but they’re all mostly power endurance, not power. Power training means low numbers of high-quality reps. Stop the set as soon as a rep is noticeably slower, shorter, or messier than the first.

Ninety seconds between sets isn’t enough. You need at least three minutes, sometimes more, for the systems that drive maximal efforts to recover. If it feels like you’re resting way more than you should and you’re slightly bored, you’re probably resting the right amount.

The intention to move as fast as possible is a large part of what drives the adaptation, even when the movement itself ends up slow. So remember, if you’re doing a power pull-up, even if only the first 20% of the pull is fast, that’s ok. You should put in full intent, with complete focus on speed for each pull, and you’ll find that’s what really counts.

Some climbers only hang, push, squeeze, and lift heavy. Others love to campus, jump, spin, and dyno. The best results come from working across the scale. Heavy strength work (for the foundation of power and speed) and explosive power work should be included in the same training block, with the emphasis shifting depending on what you need for your goals.

Below are three power exercises that work well. Pick one or two for a session, do them first, and remember to stop as soon as speed or quality reduces.

If your 1RM pull-up is greater than 160%, you may be able to do this at bodyweight.

Pull as fast as possible, aiming to get your chest as high as possible, almost as if you’re trying to roll into a muscle up. Do four sets of three, resting three minutes between sets. The faster you can complete each rep, the better, so generally use more assistance than you might think.

Always learn how to do this session on a comfortable hold and using your preferred grip type.

Using a hangboard edge (or a load cell, if you have one), pull as hard and as fast as you can for two to three seconds. Do four to eight reps, resting at least three minutes in between. This trains your ability to generate high force quickly, which is exactly what deadpoints need.

These can be done on holds, a hangboard, or a pull-up bar. The critical part is getting the height of the starting position right.

Using your chosen hold, you want to jump from the ground into the middle range of the pull-up motion, using the momentum from your lower body to assist in generating speed and power in the upper part of the pull. You’re jumping into the pull-up with your legs, and your arms and back are finishing off the pull at high speed as you’re already carrying momentum. Do four to eight reps, resting at least three minutes in between.

Train power like this two to three times a week when it’s your focus, and once or twice a week to maintain it the rest of the time. Don’t forget that power fades faster than strength when you stop training it, so it’s worth keeping a small amount in your plan all year round.

So back to Güllich: Is there such a thing as too much power? Probably not. But there’s definitely such a thing as too much fatigue, too much volume, and too little speed and intention when you’re trying to train it. Get strong first, train power fresh in mind and body, keep the quality high, and stop before it drops. I think you’ll be pretty happy with how you feel on the wall after just a couple of months of this.

Unrest grows at Rotherham United after Tuesday night developments

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Rotherham United exited the Jersey Mike's Trophy in rather disappointing fashion on Tuesday night, losing to Newcastle United's Under-21s at the AESSEAL New York Stadium.

Alex Bruce's side needed a pick-me-up in the midst of an eight-game winless run in all competitions, which has seen them slide down towards the relegation zone in League Two, and a chance to progress in the EFL Trophy would have helped provide that.

Instead, a rotated, yet still filled with experienced senior names such as Dominic Iorfa, Brandon Williams, Kian Spence and Kladi Lolos, squad put in a meek showing against an academy side, who ran away 1-0 victors and secured second place in Northern Group C.

Pick any two players. Send the code, and your mate enters it here to play the same pair.

It's not been the best of seasons so far for Rotherham back in League Two, but this particular result has soured opinion on Bruce, COO Paul Douglas and chairman Tony Stewart even further.

Rotherham weren't exactly banging on the door of Newcastle's Under-21s on Tuesday night, despite being behind for close to an hour. The Millers had just nine shots, despite their 65% possession, and the Magpies academy actually had more on target over the course of the game.

Rotherham fans have, understandably, voiced their displeasure at the performance and the result online, with the majority of the ire directed at the club's hierarchy.

One supporter posted a photo with the caption, "Stewart and Douglas, killing our club," while another tweeted, "Against a team of kids and we looked softer than them. The clubs rotten. Stewart out #rufc"

"Can Tony Stewart please exit the club now. Ruined his legacy, purely for his own ego. Stewart out," was also amongst the comments, as was "Only the trophy but wow, that might be a new low... That's saying something with the last few years."

Alex Bruce hasn't been without criticism, either, though. "Is this Bruce’s final game?" someone questioned. "Stewart out Bruce out McLaren out Douglas out anyone at board level out," was also commented.

Arguably more damaging, though, was the tweet stating that the South Yorkshire side were "dead and buried," and "the worst side in England."

It's not uncommon for sides to fall from League One to League Two and struggle in the division below. More often than not, those clubs are in for another relegation battle than they are a promotion one, and in Carlisle United and Forest Green Rovers' case, they fall through the trapdoor once again and into non-league.

Rotherham look dangerously close to being one of the next sides to do that, but you get the feeling that the writing has been on the wall for quite some time now.

The Millers have been one of the more unstable clubs in the EFL for a while, bouncing up and down from League Two to the Championship and then yo-yoing from the second and third tiers over the last decade.

Do you think Rotherham United could get relegated again this season? Let us know in the comments.

Since their double promotion to the second tier in 2014, Rotherham haven't spent more than three years in one division. Many felt that a lengthy period in League One could provide some stability, but it would only be two years before they fell back into the fourth tier.

Now, the scary possibility of falling out of the Football League is on the horizon, and a lot of Millers fans are numb to the rapid regression of the club in recent seasons.

It's been a real mess at Rotherham over the past 12 months, but it's been highlighted more so in the last six.

From Lee Clark calling out the players' commitment in the latter stages of the League One campaign last season, to the intriguing implementation of a Head of Football and then hiring Steve McClaren to take that role, to the long, drawn-out process of hiring a new head coach which landed at Alex Bruce's feet, it's not been smooth sailing.

AI has ranked the top 16 British football clubs and their hooligan firms.

Bruce came into the club just days before pre-season started, with just 14 senior players confirmed to be staying on. He's had to navigate wholesale changes in the squad and nurse a relegation hangover in what is his first EFL managerial gig.

And ultimately, the underlying numbers, especially going forward, haven't read awfully. But the new boss can't account for the effort his players put in on the pitch and some of the mindless decisions that his players have made, too.

Bruce may pay for the loss against Newcastle's kids with his job, but, ultimately, it would take a real miracle-maker to turn Rotherham's fortunes around and get the fanbase back onside.

Barry Bannan reacts as Ali Al-Hamadi drops Sheffield Wednesday message

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Sheffield Wednesday striker Ali Al-Hamadi has taken to social media to reflect on his start to life at Hillsborough, and team-mate Barry Bannan has reacted to his post.

Al-Hamadi was one of 18 new signings to join Wednesday this summer as part of the club's rebuild under new owner David Storch, but it is fair to say his loan move from Ipswich Town has not gone as well as he would have hoped so far.

It has been an inconsistent start to life back in League One for Wednesday, with the South Yorkshire outfit currently sitting ninth in the table, two points from the play-off places, after winning just two of their last nine league games.

After parting company with Henrik Pedersen last month, the Owls appointed former Stockport County boss Dave Challinor as their new manager this week, and the 51-year-old is set to take charge for the first time in Sunday's FA Cup tie at Oldham Athletic.

However, while Challinor will be keen to run the rule over his new squad over the coming weeks, he may be without Al-Hamadi for the opening games of his tenure after he suffered a fresh injury blow.

Al-Hamadi is yet to open his account for Wednesday since his summer arrival, and injuries have limited him to just eight appearances so far, with his only start coming in the 2-0 defeat to Doncaster last month which proved to be Pedersen's final game.

After missing a few games earlier in the season with a foot problem, Al-Hamadi was not in the squad for Sunday's 1-1 draw with Blackpool due to a new injury, which manager Matt Hamshaw revealed could keep him out for "a number of weeks".

Unfortunately for Al-Hamadi, he has had his fair share of injury woes in recent years, particularly during his loan spell at Luton Town last season, where he scored just one goal and provided one assist in 14 appearances.

Al-Hamadi would have been to get a consistent run of games under his belt at Hillsborough and establish himself in the Wednesday team, but despite finding himself in the treatment room once again, it seems the Iraq international is determined to bounce back from his latest setback.

"Frustratingly, it has been stop-start the last few weeks for me personally," Al-Hamadi wrote on Instagram on Tuesday.

"Working as hard as ever to stay on that pitch, help the team and reach our goals for this season."

Al-Hamadi was inundated with support after his candid reflection on his time at Wednesday, with the post attracting over 16,600 likes at the time of writing, and among those was his Owls team-mate Barry Bannan.

Al-Hamadi has proven himself to be a prolific scorer in the EFL previously during his spell in League Two with AFC Wimbledon, but he has struggled to produce his best form since departing Plough Lane for Ipswich for a fee of just over £1 million almost three years ago.

Despite his injury problems at Luton last season, it is easy to see why Wednesday took a gamble on Al-Hamadi this summer, but as he continues to be plagued by fitness issues, his loan stint at Hillsborough is in danger of turning into another flop.

Should Ali Al-Hamadi be a regular starter for Sheffield Wednesday under Dave Challinor once he returns from injury? Tell us in the comments below!

With Challinor taking over this week, Al-Hamadi would no doubt have been keen to show the new Owls boss what he is capable of, but it appears he will be unavailable for the early stages of his reign, which could allow others to move ahead of him in the pecking order.

« La vie ne se résume pas à gagner des courses cyclistes » : les confidences touchantes de Pauline Ferrand-Prévot après son année 2026 difficile

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Pauline Ferrand-Prévot est sortie de plusieurs mois de silence pour évoquer ouvertement son état d'esprit et sa santé mentale. La leader de la formation Visma | Lease a Bike a pris la parole sur ses réseaux sociaux pour annoncer le terme d'une longue pause salutaire après son abandon lors du Tour de France Femmes.

Après une saison 2025 très réussie marquée par ses sacres sur Paris-Roubaix et la Grande Boucle, la championne rémoise de 34 ans a traversé un exercice 2026 bien plus contrasté, sans victoires majeures malgré des podiums sur le Tour des Flandres et Paris-Roubaix. En difficulté pour rivaliser avec les meilleures sur La Vuelta puis contrainte à l'abandon avant le départ de la 8e étape du Tour de France Femmes cet été selon les bilans de course, Pauline Ferrand-Prévot n'avait plus épinglé le moindre dossard en compétition depuis lors.

Éprouvée par cette spirale négative, Pauline Ferrand-Prévot a décidé de s'éloigner des pelotons pour faire redescendre une immense pression sportive. Sur son compte Instagram, la représentante de Visma | Lease a Bike a expliqué combien la quête absolue du résultat avait fini par étouffer son quotidien : « J’étais tellement focalisée sur l’idée de gagner à nouveau le Tour que j’en avais perdu la joie du quotidien. Tout tournait autour de la performance, de l’exigence constante de faire toujours plus pour être la meilleure. »

Face à une saison blanche de grands trophées en dépit d'une rigueur absolue, la championne a reconnu avoir été totalement surprise par cet échec sportif : « Par le passé, j’avais déjà traversé des périodes difficiles, où je me sentais triste et perdue. Mais je n’avais jamais vécu une situation où, malgré tous mes efforts et une préparation irréprochable, je me retrouvais finalement avec rien. C’est sans doute ce qui explique pourquoi, ces derniers mois, je n’arrivais tout simplement pas à comprendre ce qui m’arrivait. » Pour surmonter cette surcharge mentale, elle s'est donc accordé une vraie coupure en famille, loin des sacrifices habituels : « Depuis le Tour, j’ai parfois repris mon vélo, tout en m’accordant des plaisirs que je m’étais interdits durant toute la saison. J’ai passé du temps avec ma famille, profité des choses simples et savouré des moments précieux, loin du vélo. »

Au-delà de la sphère sportive, les récentes événements de sa vie personnelle ont profondément redéfini l'ordre de ses priorités. Le mercredi 7 octobre 2026, son compagnon Dylan van Baarle, qui court sous les couleurs de la formation Soudal Quick-Step, a été victime d'un accident de la circulation à l'entraînement ayant nécessité une intervention chirurgicale au poignet. Cet événement traumatisant a représenté un déclic majeur pour relativiser l'importance du sport de haut niveau dans son existence.

« Cet incident m’a fait prendre conscience que la vie ne se résume pas à gagner des courses cyclistes. Voir l’être que j’aime le plus au monde traverser une épreuve aussi douloureuse m’a permis de remettre les choses en perspective. Pour une fois, j’étais 'heureuse' de pouvoir être à ses côtés et de faire tout mon possible pour le soutenir. D’habitude, c’est toujours lui qui prend soin de moi », a détaillé la coureuse tricolore. Ainsi, le couple s'est marié fin octobre 2026 aux Pays-Bas au cours d'une cérémonie intime célébrée par la mère de Dylan van Baarle. « Je me souviens avoir ressenti beaucoup de joie en remportant les Jeux olympiques, le Tour ou Paris-Roubaix, mais épouser Dylan, entourée des personnes les plus importantes de ma vie, a procuré un bonheur et un épanouissement d’une tout autre dimension », a-t-elle confié sur le réseau social. Une étape fondamentale dans sa reconstruction.

Désormais apaisée, Pauline Ferrand-Prévot ne songe absolument pas à prendre sa retraite, elle dont le contrat avec la formation Visma | Lease a Bike court jusqu'à la fin de l'année 2028 selon des informations de presse. Toutefois, la coureuse ne compte brûler aucune étape avant d'envisager son grand retour à la compétition, constatant en toute lucidité la perte de sa condition physique : « Et voilà, c’est la fin d’une très longue intersaison, dont j’avais grand besoin. »

« Cette longue intersaison m’était vraiment nécessaire. Le chemin sera long pour retrouver un niveau correct, car je ne suis plus du tout en forme. Mais, pour l’instant, l’essentiel est de retrouver le plaisir d’être sur un vélo », a enchaîné la Rémoise. D'ailleurs, Pauline Ferrand-Prévot a d'ores et déjà fixé l'échéance de sa reprise de l'entraînement dès le mois de janvier afin de préparer la saison 2027. Un travail de longue haleine qu'elle aborde avec sérénité et une réelle envie : « Je suis sûre que je reviendrai plus forte. » Le premier coup de pédale est programmé pour janvier.

Want an AI job? Deployment engineers are earning executive-level pay – up to $1.45M a year

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

AI has ushered in new jobs, and some of them are paying handsomely, according to a new study.

AI engineers are making well over six figures in salary — and plenty more in bonuses and equity, according to data from a new report by executive search firm Christian & Timbers.

Enterprises now pay “elite” AI deployment engineers between $586,000 and $1.45 million annually in total compensation, with senior leaders in the field earning up to $2.25 million, according to the report. This figure approaches the median compensation of executive officers of public companies, the authors note.

Also: ‘So perfect, they are literally identical’ – Your AI-polished resumes are recruiters’ new top stressor

Christian & Timbers is an AI executive search and leadership advisory firm, recruiting CEOs, board members, technology executives, and AI engineers for enterprises. The new report draws on more than 550 interviews with engineers, deployment leaders, and executives conducted from January through June 2026, along with closed and current open offers. The compensation bands are Christian & Timbers’ estimates and represent ranges rather than statistical averages.

“Enterprise CEOs are under pressure to deliver returns for their AI investments. The most important decision is how to build the team that delivers on that goal,” said Jeff Christian, founder and CEO of Christian & Timbers, in a press release.

Also: What workers are really using AI for in 2026 – and what they aren’t

The report noted that the staggering salary range includes base salary, bonus, and annualized equity. Christian & Timbers said it defines an “elite” forward deployment engineer (FDE) as “a senior AI-native deployment builder, typically with 7 to 15 years of experience, including at least two years building and deploying agentic automated workflows that deliver measurable ROI with a documented enterprise impact of $10M or more,” who has also led a team of other forward deployment engineers.

The large salary ranges reflect increasing enterprise investments in AI talent and employee development. Microsoft, AWS, and OpenAI have announced more than $7.5 billion in corporate and partner investment in AI deployment over the past six months, Christian & Timbers noted in the release.

Also: Nearly 70% of workers use AI regularly now – but many get no time to upskill

There’s an increasing need to fill roles such as Senior Principal AI Engineer, AI Solutions Engineer, and Head of Deployment — but there is a limited pool of qualified candidates. The firm estimates that only 2,000 of the most senior deployment leaders exist in the US market, according to a previous Christian & Timbers study, which does not meet demand — so companies are competing for the same small group of professionals, hence the increasing salaries.

While the report underscores lucrative yields for certain engineering roles, the firm said that public job titles make comparisons more challenging.

“Christian & Timbers’ elite category spans several titles, while advertised FDE roles cover a broader range of experience and responsibilities. Experienced deployment engineers may be recruited directly or promoted into the work under titles such as staff software engineer or principal AI engineer,” the study said.

Plus, equity and bonus make up 52% to 66% of senior engineer pay, and most job postings don’t include that value in the salary, the report noted.

Are High-Intensity Workouts Messing With Your Cortisol?

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

If you’ve scrolled through social media lately, you’ve probably seen wellness influencers claim high-intensity exercise increases cortisol, the infamous “stress hormone” that, in excess amounts, can lead to fatigue, weight gain, trouble sleeping, high blood pressure, and elevated blood sugar.

Many influencers say to switch to a no-stress, low-sweat regimen. And while low-intensity exercises such as Pilates, treadmill walking, or yoga are valid ways to work out—and serve as excellent cross-training activities for runners—HIIT workouts (like hill repeats, sprint intervals, and tempo runs) are key if you’re training for a race or looking to build speed and power.

But are high-intensity runs actually messing with your hormones, or is the wellness side of the internet spreading misinformation? We’re here to separate fact from fiction.

Cortisol is a hormone made by your adrenal glands, which are located on top of your kidneys and are part of your body’s endocrine system.

“Cortisol helps regulate metabolism, blood pressure, and the immune response, and it makes energy available when the body is under stress—including during exercise,” says Dr. Aysegul Eren, M.D., endocrinologist and medical director of the Adrenal Program at The Ohio State University Wexner Medical Center.

This hormone rises when your body faces increased physical or metabolic demand, such as exercise, illness, fasting, low blood sugar, or psychological stress, Eren explains. During exercise, cortisol naturally spikes as a way to provide energy to your body so it can perform. Viewed through that lens, cortisol is an important part of your body’s response to exercise and your ability to complete workouts.

“Higher-intensity exercise reliably causes a temporary cortisol rise, generally related to exercise intensity,” Eren says. “This is normal physiology, not something harmful to prevent.”

How long cortisol remains elevated depends on the stressor, according to Dr. Feyza Erenler, M.D., an endocrinologist at Tufts Medical Center. It may be minutes to hours for a brief stimulus like exercise, she explains.

A 2021 meta-analysis of 10 studies on HIIT, published in the Scandinavian Journal of Medicine & Science in Sports, found that cortisol increased immediately after exercise, often fell below baseline about two hours later, and returned to baseline within 24 hours.

“There’s no evidence that a specific number of HIIT sessions causes chronically high cortisol,” Eren says. “The more relevant concern is overtraining—chronic training without adequate recovery—which can lead to persistent underperformance. ‘Too much’ is individual, depending on training load, sleep, nutrition, recovery, and other stressors.”

In fact, research shows physical activity can actually lead to lower cortisol levels. A 2026 study in the Journal of Sport and Health Science found that those who did 150 minutes of moderate- to vigorous-intensity aerobic exercise (50 to 85% of their max heart rate) every week for a year saw a reduction in long-term cortisol levels.

Symptoms frequently attributed to “high cortisol,” such as fatigue, poor sleep, anxiety, or difficulty losing weight, don’t indicate that a person has high cortisol, according to Erenler. “These symptoms are extremely common and nonspecific and can occur for many different reasons,” she says.

Signs of true high cortisol—not the brief, self-resolving spike during exercise—include bruising, muscle weakness (particularly in the upper legs), wide purple stretch marks, worsening high blood pressure or diabetes, and sometimes changes in fat distribution, according to Eren.

If you’re experiencing any of the above symptoms, Eren recommends discussing them with your healthcare provider rather than trying to diagnose yourself with high cortisol all on your own (or with the help of a chatbot). Chronic high cortisol levels that are undiagnosed can cause high blood pressure, diabetes, muscle and bone loss, higher infection risk, and cardiovascular complications.

But rest assured, cortisol disorders are rare. For instance, Cushing syndrome, a condition where you have too much cortisol in your blood for a long period of time, only affects 10 to 15 people per million each year, according to the Endocrine Society. Cushing syndrome is caused by a tumor on the pituitary gland at the base of your brain, not too much high-intensity exercise.

“During exercise, cortisol temporarily rises in response to the physiological stress and then decreases as the body recovers and the stressor resolves,” Erenler says. “In Cushing syndrome, cortisol exposure remains inappropriately elevated over time, and the normal regulatory mechanisms—including the normal circadian pattern and suppression of cortisol production—are disrupted.”

For most healthy people, there’s no need to try to suppress the normal cortisol response to exercise. Instead, the goal should be a sustainable training program that includes adequate recovery, sufficient sleep and nutrition, and variation in workout intensity.

“Don’t worry about the temporary rise in cortisol that occurs with exercise,” Eren says. “It’s a normal part of the body’s response to physical activity, not a sign that exercise is harming you.”

Erenler agrees and says the goal isn’t to eliminate cortisol—it’s to challenge your body appropriately and give it enough recovery to adapt to the stress you’re placing on it.

A Modern Seattle Floating Home Takes Coastal Living to New Heights

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Before arriving at its permanent home in Seattle’s Eastlake floating-home community, this two-story residence took a journey of its own. Built entirely on a concrete float at Dyna Builders‘ Ballard docks, the house was towed across the lake and installed in its Eastlake slip. Now, with expansive glass walls opening directly onto the water, Modern Beach House feels firmly connected to its surroundings, even as it floats above them.

Designed by Hoshide Wanzer Architects + Interiors and constructed by Dyna Builders, the 2,123-square-foot residence combines the relaxed character of a Nantucket beach house with the clean lines and material richness of Pacific Northwest architecture. Its three bedrooms and open living spaces accommodate an active family whose daily routines revolve around swimming, boating, and entertaining.

“The homeowners were drawn to the relaxed, coastal aesthetic of Nantucket, and that influence really helped shape the character of the home,” says Ren Chandler, president and founder of Dyna Builders. Rather than relying on overt nautical motifs, the design incorporates weathered wood, tactile finishes, and a palette of blues, greens, and warm neutrals.

Constructing a house on water requires a different approach from building on solid ground. The new concrete float provides a stable foundation for the home while accommodating utilities and simplifying long-term maintenance. Building at Dyna’s Ballard docks gave the team easier access to trades and equipment while minimizing disruption to neighboring floating homes.

“Weight, balance, and structural integrity are all critical considerations, and everything needs to be carefully coordinated from the beginning,” Chandler explains. Unlike a conventional foundation, the floating structure requires precise weight distribution, influencing decisions about everything from the structural system to interior finishes.

On the main level, the kitchen, dining area, and living room share an open plan oriented toward Lake Union. Large Fleetwood glass doors slide into pockets within the fireplace massing, allowing the living spaces to extend seamlessly onto the waterfront deck. The chimney continues through both floors, functioning as an architectural spine that organizes the interior.

A deep blue sectional anchors the living room, complemented by sculptural lounge chairs and a softly textured fireplace surround. Nearby, a built-in dining banquette wrapped in patterned upholstery offers generous seating beneath a chandelier of colorful glass globes. The kitchen pairs warm wood cabinetry with white upper cabinets and dramatically veined countertops.

Durability is central to the design. Weathered yakisugi siding extends from the exterior into the stairwell, while cork-rubber flooring, stain-resistant cabinetry fronts, and resilient tile accommodate wet gear, pets, and frequent gatherings. A dedicated mudroom with interior and exterior showers provides a practical transition between the lake and the living spaces.

“Living directly on the water introduces some unique considerations, particularly when it comes to moisture, durability, and long-term maintenance,” Chandler notes. Careful detailing around exterior openings, decks, and exposed surfaces helps the home withstand the Pacific Northwest’s damp climate.

Upstairs, the primary suite opens directly onto a private deck overlooking Lake Union. Glass railings preserve the view, while a corner fireplace, upholstered headboard, and warm wood millwork create a more intimate atmosphere.

An adjacent dressing area features a particularly striking detail: a sculptural wood partition composed of vertically arranged slats with carved, undulating profiles. Integrated storage turns the partition into both a functional element and a tactile architectural feature.

The two additional bedrooms maximize their compact footprints with custom built-in bunks featuring internal steel framing fabricated in Dyna’s metal shop. One features illustrated maritime imagery, while the other incorporates botanical motifs.

The stairwell offers another example of the project’s custom craftsmanship. Illuminated resin panels form a translucent screen alongside the steel-and-wood stairs, offering a subtle nautical reference while casting a soft glow across the circulation space. Carefully positioned ribbon windows and layered window treatments address privacy within the closely spaced floating-home community without sacrificing natural light.

For Chandler, the most successful aspects of the project are not necessarily the most visible. “There’s a tremendous amount of engineering and coordination that goes into a project like this, but ideally, none of that is particularly apparent when you’re walking through the house,” he says.

That balance between technical precision and everyday comfort defines Modern Beach House. With its adaptable interiors, expressive materials, and close relationship to the lake, the residence makes an unconventional way of living feel entirely natural.

Murrye Bernard is an architect-turned-writer covering architecture, interiors, product design, and sustainability. A registered architect, she has written for Architect, Architectural Record, Metropolis, Hospitality Design, Texas Architect, Green Building Advisor, and The Architect's Newspaper. She previously served as managing editor of Contract magazine and enjoys exploring the intersection of design, materials, and culture.

Barker Associates Architecture Office pairs preserved architectural details with saturated hues, custom woodwork, and contemporary art in a four-story Clinton Hill home for empty nesters

From Drifting Lights to Crystal Grid, Preciosa’s extensive design portfolio brings the craft to customizable crystal.

Across System 1224, Sorbetti 2.0, and Muro, B+N Industries designs modular frameworks that expand, reconfigure, and take on new roles years after they first go up

You’ll always hear it from Design Milk first. Our passion is discovering and highlighting emerging talent, and we’re energized by and for our community of like-minded design lovers — like you!

Hyatt Category 4 Free Night Certificate — Card Perk Sweet Spot

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

The simplest high-value card perk in travel rewards — no points math required

Picture this: you walk into a stunning hotel lobby — maybe it's the Hyatt Regency Kyoto, maybe it's the Andaz Maui at Wailea, maybe it's a chic Hyatt Centric in the heart of a city you've always wanted to explore. You hand over your World of Hyatt card at check-in, and the night is on the house. Not because you're a high roller. Not because you spent $50,000 on a credit card. Because you crossed a spending threshold you were hitting anyway, and Hyatt rewarded you with a free night certificate that covers any Category 1–4 property in the entire portfolio. This is what the ChooseFI community means when we talk about travel rewards done right: identifying the moments where a credit card benefit perfectly intersects with your normal life, and extracting maximum value with minimum complexity. ## The Math That Makes This a No-Brainer Here's the value proposition in plain numbers. Category 4 Hyatt properties — places like the Thompson Nashville, the Hyatt Regency Maui, or the Andaz San Diego — regularly command **$200 to $400 per night** in cash. On peak dates, holiday weekends, or in premium markets, that number climbs even higher. Your free night certificate covers any of these properties at no additional points cost. None. Zero. This isn't a discount. It's a full, unrestricted free night at properties that charge real-world luxury rates. Why does this represent a genuine pricing inefficiency? Because Hyatt's Category 1–4 certificates are earned through a flat spending threshold — $15,000 on the World of Hyatt Credit Card in a calendar year — and can be redeemed at properties whose nightly rates vary enormously. A Category 4 certificate costs the same to earn whether you use it at a $150 roadside property or a $400 resort. The system rewards you for targeting the high end of the range every single time. For a household already putting everyday spending on a rewards card, $15,000 across a full calendar year is often an achievable benchmark. Groceries, utilities, insurance premiums, quarterly tax payments — it adds up faster than you think. And when it does, Hyatt drops a certificate in your account that can deliver **$300–$400 in hotel value** without spending a single additional point. In the World of Hyatt program — consistently rated the **best hotel loyalty program for value per point** — this certificate is one of the most quietly powerful perks on any hotel card in the market.

The core mechanism here isn't a transfer partnership exploit or a routing rule loophole. It's simpler and more durable than that: Hyatt's Category system creates a ceiling, not a floor. When you earn a Category 1–4 free night certificate, you're not locked into a $150-a-night budget motel. You're handed a key that opens any door in that range — and some of those doors lead to genuinely spectacular properties.

Hyatt operates on a fixed award chart, ranging from 3,500 points for a Category 1 night all the way up to 45,000 points for a Category 8 night. Unlike programs that use dynamic pricing (where award costs swing with cash demand), Hyatt's chart is static. A Category 4 property costs the same 15,000 points whether it's a Tuesday in February or New Year's Eve.

Your free night certificate functions exactly like those 15,000 points — but you earned it through card spending, not through a points balance. This means the certificate has a defined and predictable value: whatever a Category 4 property charges in cash on the night you use it is exactly what you're getting for free.

The pricing inefficiency exists because Hyatt assigns categories based on average nightly rates across the year — but peak-season rates at desirable Category 4 properties can blow well past that average. A Hyatt Regency in a major city might be Category 4 because its average annual rate qualifies, but on a conference weekend or holiday weekend, that same room might cost $350–$400. Your certificate captures that peak-season value without any upcharge.

While this sweet spot is certificate-based (no points required), World of Hyatt integrates beautifully with two major transferable currency programs:

This matters for your broader strategy. If you want to stack a certificate with a separate points redemption — say, booking multiple nights where the first is covered by the certificate and subsequent nights use transferred points — you have two strong, flexible on-ramps into the Hyatt ecosystem. Chase Ultimate Rewards is the workhorse of the FI community's travel rewards stack, and having Hyatt as an instant 1:1 partner makes it one of the most actionable transfer relationships in the game.

Hyatt charges no fuel surcharges on award redemptions — a meaningful distinction compared to some airline programs where surcharges can swallow most of your savings. Your certificate redemption is clean: one certificate, one free night, no surprise fees at checkout beyond standard resort fees (which, to be fair, do apply at some properties — more on that in Pro Tips).

World of Hyatt points expire after 24 months of account inactivity. As a World of Hyatt Credit Card holder, your card activity will naturally keep your account active, so expiry is rarely a concern for cardholders. But if you ever cancel the card, make sure you use your points and certificates before they age out.

Booking a World of Hyatt free night certificate is genuinely one of the easiest processes in all of travel rewards. Here's the complete walkthrough from certificate to check-in.

Log in to your World of Hyatt account at hyatt.com and navigate to your account dashboard. Under the "My Certificates & Awards" section, confirm your Category 1–4 free night certificate is present and note its expiration date. Certificates are typically valid for 12 months from issuance, so don't let it sit unused. If you don't see it and you've crossed the $15,000 annual spend threshold on your World of Hyatt Credit Card, allow a few days for it to post after your statement closes, or call Chase to confirm.

Go to hyatt.com and use the hotel search function. You can filter by World of Hyatt category — specifically, search for Category 4 properties in any destination you're targeting. Don't shortchange yourself by defaulting to a Category 1 or 2 property out of habit. The certificate works at any Category 1–4 property, so always target the top of the range. Look for properties with high nightly cash rates — this is where your certificate delivers the most value.

Once you've identified a target property, search your dates on hyatt.com and switch the search to "Use Points." Category 4 properties will show award availability for 15,000 points per night. If award availability shows for that date, your certificate will cover it. Hyatt's booking window opens 395 days in advance, so for peak travel dates — holidays, major events, popular travel seasons — you can and should book the maximum window out.

Proceed through the booking process. At the payment step, you'll see an option to "Apply Free Night Award" or select your certificate from your account. Select the Category 1–4 certificate. The system will apply it and your total cash charge drops to $0 for the room rate (plus taxes and any applicable resort fees).

Hyatt award availability at popular properties can be limited during peak periods. Your tactical options:

Hyatt operates on a fixed category system where each property is assigned a category from 1 to 8 based on the brand and location, not the current market rate. This creates a structural pricing inefficiency: a Category 4 property in a high-demand market like Maui or Kyoto charges the same certificate cost as a Category 4 in a mid-tier city. Your free night certificate works at all of them equally. Unlike hotel programs with dynamic pricing where the cost of a free night fluctuates with demand, Hyatt's fixed chart means you can target the highest-value Category 4 properties without paying more. The card's $95 annual fee is one of the lowest in the premium hotel card category, yet the certificate alone delivers $250 to $400 or more in value — a return that makes the math trivially easy for any FI-minded household.

The Andaz San Diego is a boutique lifestyle hotel in the Gaslamp Quarter — rooftop pool, stylish rooms, walking distance to everything downtown. It's the kind of hotel that signals a real trip, not just a place to sleep. On a standard weekend night, cash rates run $250–$350 per night. During Comic-Con, a Padres playoff weekend, or a major convention, rates push past $400.

Cash cost: $300 (conservative weekend estimate) Certificate cost: 0 points, 1 Category 1–4 free night certificate Your out-of-pocket: $0 for the room rate

You earned this certificate by crossing $15,000 in annual spending on the World of Hyatt Credit Card — a threshold many households reach through regular everyday spending over 12 months. The certificate posts automatically, you search availability on hyatt.com, and you apply it at checkout in about five minutes.

You're not redeeming into a budget experience. The Andaz brand sits at the upper-upscale tier of the Hyatt portfolio. You check in through a host model (no formal front desk), settle into a room with thoughtful design, and wake up knowing that $300 night cost you nothing extra beyond your normal spending.

If you hold World of Hyatt elite status — Discoverist, Explorist, or Globalist — that status applies to certificate bookings exactly like paid stays. Room upgrade requests, early check-in, late check-out: all the same benefits apply.

In every case, your one certificate delivers more value than most points redemptions in any hotel program. And you earned it without touching your points balance.

This sweet spot is built for the everyday FI household that's already spending $15,000 or more annually on a single credit card and wants to redirect that activity toward a meaningful travel benefit. You don't need a large points balance. You don't need to understand transfer partners or award chart math. You need: the World of Hyatt Credit Card, $15,000 in annual card spending, and a World of Hyatt account. No minimum points balance required — this is a card perk, not a points redemption.

If your annual spending is significantly below $15,000 on a single card, this particular mechanism won't trigger. In that case, the World of Hyatt Credit Card still earns Hyatt points on spending and delivers a Category 1–4 certificate on each card anniversary — so it retains value, just through a different path. Also, if you're deeply committed to a non-Hyatt hotel program, the benefit is obviously less useful without a library of Hyatt properties in your regular rotation.

## Rated: Easy (4/5 Beginner Friendly) This is one of the most accessible sweet spots in the entire travel rewards ecosystem, and that's by design. The certificate mechanism removes the two biggest friction points in traditional points redemptions: **finding the right number of points** and **navigating complex transfer timing**. You earn the certificate passively through spending, it posts to your account automatically, and the booking process on hyatt.com is as intuitive as booking any other hotel stay. ## What Makes It Easy - **No transfer required** — the certificate lives in your World of Hyatt account, ready to apply at checkout - **Fixed pricing** — Category 4 is Category 4 everywhere in the world; no dynamic pricing surprises - **Simple availability search** — hyatt.com's award search is clean and straightforward ## The One Genuine Challenge: Availability at Popular Properties The only real friction point is award inventory at high-demand Category 4 properties on desirable dates. Hyatt limits how many award rooms it releases per night, and popular properties in destinations like Hawaii or New York fill quickly. **Three ways to beat this:** 1. Search 395 days in advance when fresh inventory releases 2. Build a shortlist of 3–5 Category 4 properties in your target region so you have backup options 3. Stay flexible on dates — midweek nights almost always have more award availability than weekends If you can approach this with even modest date flexibility, you will find great options. The Hyatt portfolio at Category 4 is large and geographically diverse, and there is almost always a compelling property available somewhere you'd actually want to go. For anyone new to travel rewards, this certificate is the perfect first redemption — low complexity, high reward, and a genuinely memorable hotel stay to show your household what this strategy can deliver.

FEATURE | Jobe Bellingham: How a change of role has unlocked the Englishman

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

One of the biggest talking points after the 2-2 Klassiker draw between Bayern Munich and Borussia Dortmund was Jobe Bellingham’s amazing performance. The 21-year-old Englishman put on a solid display that proved he has adapted well to football at the Black and Yellows.

Following his €31m move from Sunderland in the summer of 2025, Jobe didn’t hit the ground running. He struggled to make an impact in games and didn’t look comfortable in the role he was assigned. Of course, comparisons with his older brother Jude didn’t help. The now Real Madrid player spent three seasons at the Signal Iduna Park and earned plaudits for his scintillating form.

When Jobe arrived in 2025, two years after his brother left Dortmund, expectations were high. However, it would take time for the younger Bellingham to make his mark under Niko Kovac.

Speaking to South African broadcaster SABC Sport after the recent Der Klassiker, Jobe discussed how a change in role helped him put on a good performance this season. He noted that he was able to get forward more, which helped his output.

Great clarity here from Jobe Bellingham about Dortmund's set-up and his different role in last night's 2-2 draw against Bayern Munich at the Allianz Arena…. @SABC_Sport #SABCSportinGermany ⚽ #DerKlassiker #SizenzaZonke pic.twitter.com/RXFJ4brVZZ

“Obviously, we played a different shape. You have to adapt to what your role is asking you to do. I was able to get forward a lot more. Probably get on the ball in good areas. And try to affect the last line as well as get back and help defend,” said Jobe.

Jobe has two Bundesliga assists in eight games this season. Last season he had the same number of assists in 32 games. And he almost found his first Bundesliga goal against Bayern on Saturday, but it was disallowed.

For most of last season, Jobe was paired with Felix Nmecha and had to drop deep to help the defence and control play. Now the presence of summer signing Joey Veerman has helped in that aspect, which has allowed Jobe to have more freedom.

In his first interview as a Borussia Dortmund player, Jobe said: “In the short term: simply to have a positive influence on the club. To build a connection with the fans, within my team and also with the coaching staff. I think that should be the first step for every player at every club. For me, that’s the foundation for the future. Once I’ve settled in and can start performing and doing what I can do, then the rest will take care of itself.”

It now looks like things are falling into place. Jobe is slowly growing into his role in Dortmund and becoming the player that the club wants. As the season progresses, Dortmund fans will be hoping that his good form can earn the team.

4 Things You’re Doing in Yoga That Might Worsen Lower Back Discomfort

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Monica Bright is a yoga teacher whose continuing education centered on injury mechanics and movement. She breaks down the common yoga mistakes that could be worsening lower back discomfort, and offers options for relief. You can also learn more about yoga poses for your lower back.

Lower back discomfort is one of the most common pain points among people worldwide. If you fall into that category and also do yoga, chances are you’ve expected the practice to bring you relief. But certain movements in yoga, and how you approach them, can sometimes have the opposite effect of the lower back release that you were hoping to achieve.

Learning to recognize what triggers tightness in your lower back can help you make more supportive choices on and off the mat. That way, you’re still working toward undoing your discomfort, but without accidentally worsening it.

Whether you’re in class or at home, or even moving throughout your everyday life, notice whether any of the following approaches to movement worsens your lower back sensitivity—and explore the following alternatives that might help relieve your discomfort.

Some people’s lower back strain is aggravated by moving too quickly. This is often the case with forward rounding. Think Seated Forward Bend or reaching down to pick something up off the floor. Moving slowly allows you to pay attention to the sensations in your body and ensures you’re not accidentally forcing yourself to move too far into a stretch and tweak your lower back.

What to try instead: Throughout your yoga practice, and even during your day, ask yourself, “How does it feel when I move more slowly?”

The “neutral” position of your spine that you commonly hear yoga teachers mention includes several curves, including one in the lumbar spine that is concave, meaning it curves inward. But many students have a tendency to round the lower back during certain poses, such as forward bends and Chair Pose, which can have the effect of flattening the lower spine and causing tension.

What to try instead: Maintaining that neutral spine can be more beneficial than “flattening” it for some students with a track record of stiffness and soreness in the lower back. That comes into play in several common yoga poses.

In Chair Pose, for instance, there is a tendency to draw the core toward the spine so much that it counteracts the natural curve in the lower back. Maintaining a neutral spine means keeping the length in your lower back rather than tucking the tailbone.

In Standing Forward Bend, keeping a neutral spine means being aware if you are rounding your lower back as you reach forward and, instead, hinging at your hips, perhaps bending your knees, and not being concerned with how far forward you fold.

However, some students find a greater sense of ease in the lower back by rounding forward. Ask yourself, “Does my lower back feel better, or worse, with rounded forward folds?”

If your lower back is sensitive, your yoga practice might look different from the person’s on the mat next to you—and that’s okay. The goal in yoga isn’t to push past discomfort, but to find the alignment in which your lower back feels good. And sometimes that means taking variations.

What to try instead: Explore variations of poses—some of which might include using props, such as blocks—to help support your movements. Take Happy Baby, for example. Drawing your knees toward your chest and holding the backs of your thighs might feel more supportive to some. When sitting in a forward bend, placing a blanket underneath the lowest part of your back to slightly elevate your hips and provide more room for your lower back might be your preferred version. Finding the movement options that feel tolerable for you can be enormously helpful while you’re learning to prioritize your lower back.

Stress, poor sleep, a sense of being overwhelmed, even anxiety surrounding practicing certain movements can put your body on high alert and cause your nervous system to brace for impact. This has the potential to turn up the volume on physical discomfort. Understanding how to downregulate your nervous system, on the other hand, can help your entire body relax.

What to try instead: You’re likely familiar with some of the practices that activate your parasympathetic nervous system, including resting in poses such as Child’s Pose, Legs Up the Wall, or Crocodile and practicing various pranayama (breathwork) techniques, including Box Breathing or simply lengthening your exhalations. Even slowing the pace of your practice and taking the variations that feel good to you are ways of signaling to your nervous system that you’re safe. Experiment with one or two calming exercises prior to your next yoga class or at-home practice.

Practicing with these ideas in mind will ultimately help you recognize how to support your body when practicing yoga. This doesn’t mean you need to have to have all of the answers. It simply, but it does means that you are present, aware, and listening to your own body’s signals.

Three games, three goals, three wins: Midfield maverick stars again as Norwich City thrash Derby County

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Norwich City cruised past Derby County with much of the hard work done by half time. NCFC were 3-0 up at the break, and Jovon Makama lashed home a stunning striker to seal the victory in the second half.

It was another strong team performance for the Canaries, as displayed by the fact there were four different goalscorers. However, one man who stood out and got himself on the scoresheet once again was attacking midfielder Paris Maghoma.

Maghoma had Rams midfielder Alex Mowatt biting on every dummy before he fired home from outside the box for the Canaries’ second goal of the night. It made it three goals in three consecutive wins for the maverick playmaker, who looks to be back at his best.

Maghoma made an impression early on in his Norwich City career, scoring in his first starts in the FA Cup and Championship after joining in January. His trickery and flair has frustrated even manager Philippe Clement at times, but it isn’t easy to curtail someone as expressive and skilful as the 25-year-old.

The attacking midfielder was influential in the Derby County win. Alongside his goal, Maghoma created one chance and one big chance, also completed five passes into the final third (Fotmob).

Maghoma had only started two Championship games before his recent three-game scoring and starting run. His per 90 stats show just how impactful he has been when on the pitch though, offering a creative streak and valuable abilities in progressing the ball through passes and dribbling.

Across his 442 league minutes, the NCFC ace is averaging 2.44 chances created, 6.11 line-breaking passes, 57.83 touches and a 60% dribble success rate per 90. His three goals from 10 shots and just 0.87 xG shows how clinical he has been when given an opportunity at goal too – although that does beg the question whether such goalscoring form will continue (all stats via Fotmob).

Maghoma has been a standout player in Norwich City’s three-game winning run, and there will be confidence that he can continue in this vein. However, both he and the entire team face a challenge in the effort to maintain their form.

The last three games have been against Derby County, Watford and Charlton Athletic – three of the league’s worst form teams. If NCFC are to keep pushing up the table and come good on their promotion ambitions, they have to maintain these high standards against tougher opposition.

A trip to Michael Skubala’s Bristol City marks a step up for the Canaries, but it is another winnable match. It would be a real morale boost to enter the upcoming international break off the back of four wins, and Maghoma’s recent form indicates he will have a key part to play in continuing the run.

"Keep talking about it": Raw Power Games call for Wishlist aid as they, and their historical strategy sandbox Chronicles: Medieval, lose funding

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Raw Power Games, an independent studio comprising veteran developers of The Witcher 3, Total War: Three Kingdoms, and Crusader Kings III (among others), have called on players for support after losing their funding - an existential threat to both the organisation and to their debut, the still-in-development historical strategy game Chronicles: Medieval.

In a statement posted last week, Raw Power announced that "the necessary funding" behind the studio was no longer in place, despite Chronicles: Medieval – which was unveiled last year with an expensive-looking, Tom Hardy-narrated trailer – apparently being within touching distance of its planned early access launch. Hardy or no Hardy, however, the statement declared that they aren't "going down without a fight", hinting at talks with what could be possible new investors, asking interested would-be fans to "keep talking about" the game, and encouraging its inclusion on Steam Wishlists.

A few days on, the impact of this rallying call remains unclear. Raw Power Games have made no further statements, and Chronicles: Medieval does not seem to appear on Steam's own list of Top Wishlisted games. SteamDB have it at #26 in their desire-o-rankings, representing no change from its position immediately before the funding announcement was made, and while the game's Steam follower count has increased by over 1,000 week-on-week – significantly more so than surrounding games in the list – that doesn't feel like the kind of wild grassroots support that might save a dozens-strong studio.

Still, as my childhood television used to say, every little helps, and I'm inclined to hope for Raw Power's survival if only as an antidote to the enthusiasm-poisining dread of seeing so many other developers being laid off and shut down. Chronicles: Medieval looks promisingly ambitious too, mashing up sprawling Mount & Blade-style battles with an RPG-flavoured strategy layer that involves the player's custom-created character ascending the snooty social hierarchy of 14th century England. Best of luck to everyone trying to get it over the early access line.

Roma to pursue Kvernadze in January

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Roma continues to monitor Giorgi Kvernadze, the Georgian striker currently playing for Frosinone.

The player was reportedly in the Roman club’s sights last summer and will continue to be monitored ahead of the upcoming transfer window.

This interest could become more concrete in the coming months, especially given the player’s financial terms.

The 2003-born striker is tied to Frosinone by a deal valid until June 30, 2028, but his contract reportedly contains a specific release clause.

The fee required to release the striker is reportedly €4 million, with the option to exercise the option next summer.

This valuation could make the Georgian an attractive prospect for Roma, who are keen to monitor various prospects to strengthen their attacking line.

However, the potential deal would present another financial aspect to consider.

As reported by Gazzetta Regionale, in addition to the €4 million stipulated in the release clause, a significant percentage would need to be considered for the player’s agent.

This detail could impact the overall cost of the deal and the Roma management’s assessment.

Kvernadze’s agent is the same one representing Khvicha Kvaratskhelia, another Georgian talent who has established himself in our Serie A. At the moment, Roma’s interest remains to be monitored, and no official negotiations have been initiated to bring Kvernadze to the capital.

Opinion: Liverpool lining up surprise star to replace Alisson Becker

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Liverpool are reportedly lining up a surprise move to replace Alisson Becker.

Everything comes to an end. That is the reality of life. It's why life is both beautiful and tragic at the same time. Endings make you appreciate the present, the moments for what they really were - poignant periods of our lives that had a profound impact on us.

Experiences are the greatest tokens we have as humans.

For Liverpool supporters, Alisson brought so many of these profound moments. From incredible saves to standing up in the face of tragedy.

That goal against West Brom. Where he leaped from the sky and thundered the ball into the back of the net with his forehead merely days after losing his father will live long in the memory.

It was during the darkest period of the Covid-19 Pandemic and it brought fans a split-second relief from the doom and gloom.

Alisson's time is coming to an end at LiverpoolWe've seen a lot of legends leave Anfield over the last few years, and Alisson is likely going to be the next one who follows suit. The Brazilian could have left Liverpool last summer, but in the end, the club decided to keep him. He now has less than a year left on his contract with Liverpool, which expires at the end of the season. It's hard to see him being retained any further. He's 34-years-old and he is no longer the incredible goalkeeper that he once was. Liverpool have also very clearly paved the way for his departure. They signed Giorgi Mamardashvili two years ago, brought in Armin Pecsi a year ago and also broke the world record for an U23 goalkeeper when they agreed to sign Lucca Brughmann last summer.
